Hospital financial assistance: results

31 hospitals · generated 2026-11-14 · prompt v7 · model us.moonshotai.kimi-k3 · explanations draft-1 (draft, not yet reviewed)

Open items (unclear, or a document we could not find) count as not met for the letter until a person checks them; the letter is then marked provisional. Results are given a known starting page for each hospital, from documents collected automatically. Letters: B 11, C 4, F 16. Status: graded 14, provisional 17.

Some documents were collected even though the hospital's site asks automated tools not to fetch them (robots.txt); this was a deliberate decision (Nov 12, 2026), and those hospitals are marked. Hospitals marked: 10.

Some pages and documents were collected with a headless web browser because plain downloads could not read them (pages built by JavaScript, or access checks a browser passes without a CAPTCHA). The browser names itself as automated and never solves a CAPTCHA; this was a deliberate decision (Nov 14, 2026), and those hospitals are marked. Hospitals marked: 7.

No separate physician-practice documents were provided, so nothing was set aside. B: conflicts found (see consistency_points) - poverty_figures: the summary's table is based on the 2025 Federal Poverty Guidelines ($62,600 top limit for one person) while the posted application still prints the 2024 table ($60,240 at 400% for one person); discount_levels: policy Appendix II lets charges for 301-400% FPG patients rise from 20% up to the full Medicaid amount while the application caps them at 20% of the Medicaid amount; contact_details: the summary gives Interfaith help at 718-613-4359 while the application gives Interfaith 718-613-4000.

C: those figures equal neither the official 2025 figures ($31,300/$46,950/$62,600) nor the 2026 figures ($31,920/$47,880/$63,840); they are the 2024 guidelines. D: heading and figures agree with each other (both 2024), but they are not the 2025 or 2026 guidelines.

Application

N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f)
Model's reasoning

B: no dollar conversion needed since percentages are given; the summary's dollar figure matches the 2025 400% amount. C: yes - 400%, and the policy adds OBH may offer discounts above 400%, which only extends the reach.

Service area drawn by the state's rules
Materials On Website
NOT_FOUND · open: counted as not met until a person checks it
We could not find the document needed to check this, so we cannot say whether the hospital meets this requirement. This does not mean the hospital fails it. A person will check it by hand. Until then, the grade counts it as not met.
Rule: The law says a hospital must give financial assistance for necessary, non-emergency care to patients who live in its "primary service area", and that the State Health Commissioner sets the rules for drawing that area. Under the state's 2007 rules, the area must include at least the hospital's own county and every county that borders it; for a New York City hospital, all five boroughs and the counties that border them. A hospital may not draw its area to leave out poorer or uninsured communities.
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f); N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f)(i)
Model's reasoning

A: no document defines a Primary Service Area for financial assistance - neither a list of counties/towns/zip codes nor any residence rule (e.g., 'residents of New York State') for FAP eligibility appears in the policy, the summary, the guidelines or the web pages. The only residence wording concerns insurance programs, not the FAP ('This program is for New York residents' - Medicaid; 'Are New York State residents' - Qualified Health Plans). B-G: not reached. NOT_FOUND.

Service area drawn by the state's rules
Materials On Website
NOT_FOUND · open: counted as not met until a person checks it
We could not find the document needed to check this, so we cannot say whether the hospital meets this requirement. This does not mean the hospital fails it. A person will check it by hand. Until then, the grade counts it as not met.
Rule: The law says a hospital must give financial assistance for necessary, non-emergency care to patients who live in its "primary service area", and that the State Health Commissioner sets the rules for drawing that area. Under the state's 2007 rules, the area must include at least the hospital's own county and every county that borders it; for a New York City hospital, all five boroughs and the counties that border them. A hospital may not draw its area to leave out poorer or uninsured communities.
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f); N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f)(i)
Model's reasoning

A: no document defines a Primary Service Area. Eligibility is stated solely by income and insurance status. B-G: not reached because no PSA is defined. Answer NOT_FOUND.

Documents agree with each other
Materials On Website
NO · not met
This hospital's documents contradict each other on at least one point.
9 points compared across the documents: 3 agree, 4 conflict; 2 not stated in two documents. Conflict on income limit: “Individuals up to 400% of the federal poverty level are eligible for financial assistance.” (page 5) versus “With No insuance 0-300% 100% 4 With insurance 0-200% 100%” (page 2). Conflict on discount levels: “Up to 400% FPL (Tier 1 & Tier 4) 100% Adjustment to Patient Balance” (page 5) versus “With No insuance 0-300% 100% 4 With insurance 0-200% 100%” (page 2). Conflict on immigration status or residency: “Check List – ALL APPLICANTS MUST BE LEGAL RESIDENTS OF NYS” (page 1) versus “Regardless of immigration status, if you reside in NY State, you can apply for financial assistance.” (page 1). Conflict on contact details: “If you need assistance with this application, please contact Ellis Medicine’s financial assistance program at 518.831.8123” (page 4) versus “Please call our Financial Advocates at 518.243.3460.” (page 1). document
Rule: The law says a hospital's financial assistance policy must be clear and easy to understand. Documents that contradict each other are neither. This review checks that the policy, the summary and the application say the same thing on points such as who qualifies, deadlines and how to apply.
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(c)
Model's reasoning

Consistency check: conflict on income_limit (1b776c1b p.5 vs e0d53d8d p.2); conflict on discount_levels (1b776c1b p.5 vs e0d53d8d p.2); conflict on immigration_residency (1b776c1b p.1 vs e0d53d8d p.1); conflict on contact_details (1b776c1b p.4 vs e0d53d8d p.1); 3 point(s) agree. Model answered NO: A: relevant documents compared: the Billing/Financial Assistance web page (02d40d02), the Explanation of Charges page (46cb43a1), the Participating Insurance Listing (12ad88b2), the Reading Your Bill page (34e3df7b), the English application (1b776c1b), its Spanish translation (465ccbc8, a translation of the same form), and the Financial Assistance FAQs and Plain Language Summary (e0d53d8d). B: conflicts found (see consistency_points): (1) immigration/residency - the application's cover checklist requires 'ALL APPLICANTS MUST BE LEGAL RESIDENTS OF NYS' while the summary says 'Regardless of immigration status, if you reside in NY State, you can apply for financial assistance' and the application itself (p.4) says 'immigration status shall not be an eligibility criterion'; (2) income limit/discount levels - the application gives a '100% Adjustment to Patient Balance' up to 400% FPL while the summary's income tiers give the 100% rate only at 0-300% FPL (no insurance) and 0-200% FPL (with insurance); (3) contact details - the application gives 518.831.8123 for application help while the summary and billing page give Financial Advocates 518.243.3460. Answer NO.

Model's reasoning

C: the numbers/rules do NOT all match: the state form's Minimum Discount Rates (waive all charges below 200% FPL; sliding scale up to 10% of the Medicaid amount at 200-300% FPL and up to 20% at 301-400% FPL, with underinsured cost-sharing caps) is replaced by Ellis's own schedule, 'Up to 400% FPL (Tier 1 & Tier 4) / 100% Adjustment to Patient Balance', which references Ellis's internal tiers. D: other changes: the 6-person 400% FPL figure is misprinted '$17,744' (should be $177,440); 'medically necessary services' is reworded to 'medical services'; the sentence that the application must be printed in the primary languages of patients served is absent; the 'Self' row of the family table and 'Signed statement of no income' are reworded; and a cover checklist plus a zero-income certification form are attached. Although Ellis's discount is more generous, a rule/number of the state form (the minimum discount schedule) is changed. Answer NO. B: no - no document says assets are counted; the billing web page lists decision factors 'income level, existing debts, medical situation, and other indicators of inability to pay' - debts are liabilities, not assets. Answer YES (quote shows the required-documents passage examined).

“Financial Assistance is available for uninsured and underinsured individuals who meet the income and household size guidelines.” document page 1 (collected with a headless browser)
Rule: The law says a hospital must give financial assistance for necessary, non-emergency care to patients who live in its "primary service area", and that the State Health Commissioner sets the rules for drawing that area. Under the state's 2007 rules, the area must include at least the hospital's own county and every county that borders it; for a New York City hospital, all five boroughs and the counties that border them. A hospital may not draw its area to leave out poorer or uninsured communities.
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f); N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f)(i)
Model's reasoning

The wording "This program is for New York residents" and "Are New York State residents" concerns Medicaid and Qualified Health Plans (insurance enrollment), not the hospital's financial assistance. B: nothing to list. Because no PSA definition appears anywhere, the answer is NOT_FOUND; the 2007-guideline checks (C-G) cannot be performed.

N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(c)
Model's reasoning

B: conflicts found - income_limit: the FAQ says assistance is for 'household incomes less than the federal poverty level' while the policy, summary and program page say up to 500% of FPG (the application states the 400% statutory floor); contact_details: for Mather Hospital's financial assistance department the policy and summary give 75 North Country Road, 1st Floor, Port Jefferson / 631-313-7712, but the contact page gives 100 Highlands Blvd Suite 302, Port Jefferson / (631) 476-2801, and the 2 Huntington Quadrangle office is 1-800-995-5727 in the policy but (631) 313-7700 on the contact page; how_to_apply: the policy requires prior year's tax return(s), a minimum of two pay stubs and three bank statements, while the application asks for one pay stub (or an employer letter or tax return). Rule: Under the law, who qualifies depends on income: patients with income below 400% of the federal poverty level are presumed to qualify. The law sets no test of savings or property, and the state's application form does not ask about them. The application process must not be unduly burdensome.
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f); N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(e)
Model's reasoning

B: the policy does not say assets are counted in the eligibility formula itself, but requesting bank account statements is evidence of an asset check per the criterion (CSSNY). NO.

Service area drawn by the state's rules
Materials On Website
UNCLEAR · open: counted as not met until a person checks it
The hospital's documents did not let us decide this for certain. A person will check it by hand. Until then, the grade counts it as not met.
“Jones also provides Financial Assistance to patients, who reside in our primary service areas, including Allegany, Cattaraugus and Stueben counties, and receive medically necessary inpatient or outpatient services that a physician, exercising prudent clinical judgment, would order or provide for a patient.” document page 1
Rule: The law says a hospital must give financial assistance for necessary, non-emergency care to patients who live in its "primary service area", and that the State Health Commissioner sets the rules for drawing that area. Under the state's 2007 rules, the area must include at least the hospital's own county and every county that borders it; for a New York City hospital, all five boroughs and the counties that border them. A hospital may not draw its area to leave out poorer or uninsured communities.
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f); N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f)(i)
Model's reasoning

A: yes - the policy defines the area: New York State residents for emergency services, and 'our primary service areas, including Allegany, Cattaraugus and Stueben counties' for non-emergency medically necessary services. B: the policy names Allegany, Cattaraugus and 'Stueben' (as spelled in the document) counties; the summary (559562f2) offers New York State residents emergency discounts and refers to 'the Hospital's primary service area' for non-emergency care without naming it. Per the reading rules the county list is what is judged for non-emergency care. C: the non-emergency area is not statewide, so I continue. D: no document states which county Jones Memorial Hospital is in - the only address given anywhere is the UR Medicine Affiliate Billing Office at 73 Buffalo St, Canandaigua, which is the billing office, not the hospital, so I cannot tell the hospital's county from the documents. E/F/G: without the hospital's county I cannot say whether its own county or every bordering county is included. Answer UNCLEAR; the counties named are Allegany, Cattaraugus and 'Stueben'. The DOH chart of assigned non-contiguous counties was not provided, so those were not checked.

Rule: The law says a hospital must give financial assistance for necessary, non-emergency care to patients who live in its "primary service area", and that the State Health Commissioner sets the rules for drawing that area. Rule: The law says a hospital's financial assistance policy must be clear and easy to understand. Documents that contradict each other are neither. This review checks that the policy, the summary and the application say the same thing on points such as who qualifies, deadlines and how to apply.
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(c)
Model's reasoning

Consistency check: conflict on poverty_figures (9a43c2aa p.2 vs e84c4540 p.9); conflict on discount_levels (9a43c2aa p.4 vs e84c4540 p.11); conflict on time_to_apply (9a43c2aa p.4 vs e84c4540 p.4); conflict on how_to_apply (9a43c2aa p.4 vs e84c4540 p.4); conflict on contact_details (9a43c2aa p.1 vs e84c4540 p.4); 3 point(s) agree. Set aside: 55456278 and b3f0073f (School of Nursing student financial aid, a separate program) and f619ff10 (system page for the Moses/Einstein/Wakefield/Westchester Square/MMG campuses; it names Montefiore Mount Vernon only as a referral link and says nothing substantive about it). B: conflicts found - time_to_apply: summary says 'You will have 30 days to complete the application' while the policy says there is no deadline; poverty_figures: the additional-person 300% figure is $17,040 in the summary but $17,400 in the policy, both labeled 2026; discount_levels: summary says adult outpatient/ER amounts 'start from $15' while the policy charges $0 below 200% FPL; how_to_apply/contact_details: the same office at 12 North 7th Avenue is zip 10550 in the summary and 10551 in the policy. Also noted: the summary promises a decision letter 'within 30 days' while the policy allows '30 business days'.

“You will have 30 days to complete the application.” document page 4 (collected despite robots.txt)
Rule: The law says patients can apply for financial assistance at any time, even after a bill has gone to collections. A hospital cannot set a deadline.
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(e)
Model's reasoning

A: yes - the summary states 'You will have 30 days to complete the application.' B: this appears in the 'How do I get a discount?' section and concerns completing/submitting the application, not paying a bill or collection. However, the policy states 'There is no deadline for when a patient can request to complete a financial aid application.' The summary (dated 06/03/2026) and the policy (revised 04/26, approved June 3, 2026) bear effectively the same date and directly disagree, and I cannot tell which is newer or controls.

Monthly payments capped at 5% of income
Application
NO · not met
This hospital's application does not state the 5% limit on monthly payments, or it states a higher limit.
“The hospital may request you submit documentation as proof of paid medical expenses.” document page 6 (collected despite robots.txt)
Rule: The law says that a patient paying in installments cannot be asked to pay more than 5% of their monthly income before taxes each month, and that interest is capped at 2%. The state's application form states the 5% limit; this review checks that the hospital's application does too.
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(d); N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(d)
Model's reasoning

A: the application (9a43c2aa pages 5-6) does not describe installment or payment plans at all. B and C: no cap on monthly payments is stated in the application. The only payment-plan statement is in the summary (page 3), which says 'the monthly payment will not exceed ten percent of your monthly income' - that is a different figure and, in any case, only the application counts for this criterion. NO.

Service area drawn by the state's rules
Materials On Website
NO · not met
This hospital's service area, as its documents describe it, leaves out at least one county the state's rules require.
“Olean General Hospital hutoa msaada wa kifedha kwa huduma za matibabu ya lazima kwa wagonjwa wanaoishi ndani ya eneo lake la huduma ya msingi, linalojumuisha Kaunti ya Cattaraugus na jamii za karibu.” document page 1 (collected despite robots.txt)
Rule: The law says a hospital must give financial assistance for necessary, non-emergency care to patients who live in its "primary service area", and that the State Health Commissioner sets the rules for drawing that area. Under the state's 2007 rules, the area must include at least the hospital's own county and every county that borders it; for a New York City hospital, all five boroughs and the counties that border them. A hospital may not draw its area to leave out poorer or uninsured communities.
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f); N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f)(i)
Model's reasoning

A: yes - the summary (Swahili 008a5949; Arabic 5ac6a18d says the same) defines the PSA: financial assistance for medically necessary (non-emergency) services is limited to residents of the hospital's primary service area, 'which includes Cattaraugus County and surrounding communities'; emergency assistance is available regardless of residence. B: only the summary defines the area; both translations give the same one. The policy and application name no service area. C: the area is not all of New York State and not the five NYC counties. D: the hospital is at 515 Main Street, Olean, NY (application header); Olean is in Cattaraugus County, and the summary names Cattaraugus County as the hospital's PSA. E: not applicable (not a NYC hospital). F: the hospital's own county (Cattaraugus) is named. G: the counties bordering Cattaraugus County in New York - Allegany, Chautauqua, Erie and Wyoming - are not named anywhere; the definition says only 'Cattaraugus County and surrounding communities', and 'surrounding communities' does not name the required counties. NO: the PSA omits bordering counties I am certain of (at least Allegany and Chautauqua). The DOH chart of assigned non-contiguous counties was not provided, so those were not checked. Counties named are reported in psa_counties.

Rule: Under the law, who qualifies depends on income: patients with income below 400% of the federal poverty level are presumed to qualify. The law sets no test of savings or property, and the state's application form does not ask about them. The application process must not be unduly burdensome.

The application itself (1a06f5a4 p5) lists only income proof. B: per CSSNY, a request for bank account statements is evidence of an asset check. Answer NO.

“To apply for financial assistance, please download and complete the following document:” document page 1
Rule: The law says a hospital must give financial assistance for necessary, non-emergency care to patients who live in its "primary service area", and that the State Health Commissioner sets the rules for drawing that area. Under the state's 2007 rules, the area must include at least the hospital's own county and every county that borders it; for a New York City hospital, all five boroughs and the counties that border them. A hospital may not draw its area to leave out poorer or uninsured communities.

Service area drawn by the state's rules
Materials On Website
NOT_FOUND · open: counted as not met until a person checks it
We could not find the document needed to check this, so we cannot say whether the hospital meets this requirement. This does not mean the hospital fails it. A person will check it by hand. Until then, the grade counts it as not met.
“To apply for financial assistance, please download and complete the following document: Financial Assistance Program Application” document page 1
Rule: The law says a hospital must give financial assistance for necessary, non-emergency care to patients who live in its "primary service area", and that the State Health Commissioner sets the rules for drawing that area. Under the state's 2007 rules, the area must include at least the hospital's own county and every county that borders it; for a New York City hospital, all five boroughs and the counties that border them. A hospital may not draw its area to leave out poorer or uninsured communities.
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f); N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f)(i)
Model's reasoning

A: no - no provided document defines a Primary Service Area for Elizabethtown Community Hospital. B: nothing to list. C-E: moot. Since neither a county list nor residency wording appears anywhere, the answer is NOT_FOUND; no counties are reported in psa_counties.

No test of savings or property
Materials On Website
NO · not met
This hospital's documents ask about savings or other assets, for example bank statements, when deciding who gets help.
“If unemployed, documentation showing how you support yourself/family (e.g., savings account, odd jobs)” document page 1
Rule: Under the law, who qualifies depends on income: patients with income below 400% of the federal poverty level are presumed to qualify. The law sets no test of savings or property, and the state's application form does not ask about them. The application process must not be unduly burdensome.
N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(f); N.Y. Public Health Law § 2807-k(9-a)(e)
Model's reasoning

A: yes - the List of Required Documents asks an unemployed applicant for 'documentation showing how you support yourself/family (e.g., savings account, odd jobs)' (df166714 p1), i.e., documentation that can be a savings account. Asking for bank/savings account documentation is evidence of an asset check. B: the policy and summary say assets are not counted ('WMCHN shall not consider assets when determining eligibility for Financial Assistance.', 79109072 p2; d88c558c p1), and the application itself asks only for proof of income, but the required-documents list's savings-account request is evidence of an asset check, so the answer is NO.
